# BUILD-STATE — the build-session continuity ledger

Where the last build session for each module left off. A new session READS this at orient
(`BUILD-LOOP.md` §0.a) and UPDATES it at close (`BUILD-LOOP.md` §7). This is the kit's
"where did we leave off" registry — the build-loop analog of research-sdd's FOCUSES +
RESEARCH-STATE registries, collapsed into ONE file because the build corpus is a handful of
modules, not sixty focuses.

## How to read this file

- **One `build-state.v1` envelope per module**, between `<!-- build-state.v1 -->` and
  `<!-- /build-state.v1 -->`. Fields are `key: value`; a parser strips blank lines, the
  `open_issues:` list items, and any trailing `# comment`.
- **The kit block's per-change parts are GENERATED — never hand-edit them.** `last_change:`, the `open_issues:`
  items and the session log after `last_session:` in the `kit` envelope come from `ledger.d/*.md`, one fragment
  per change (`ledger.d/<YYYY-MM-DD>-<slug>.md`: `<!-- ledger: kit -->`, `<!-- date: … -->`, optional
  `<!-- last-change-note: … -->`, one `log: YYYY-MM-DD · …` line, any `open_issue: …` lines). Run
  `toolbelt/gen-build-state.sh` after adding one; `--check` (CI, `sweep-build-state.sh`) fails a stale file.
  `last_change` = the newest fragment, items newest first, log oldest first. Entries from before fragments live
  in `ledger.d/0000-archive-pre-2026-10-23.md`. Module blocks and the other kit fields stay hand-kept.
  `[ev: retro ledger-fragments Δ1]`
- **GATED vs DECLARED — never label a gate that cannot run.**
  - **GATED** (`retro_required`, `retro_pending`): machine-enforced, but ONLY for KIT-local
    changes inside the `niagara-tools` repo — that is the only diff the enforcement check
    (build-n4-retro-gate / PR2) can see.
  - **DECLARED** (`module_repo`, `module_root`, `last_build`, `bytecode_major`, `signed`,
    `verify_gate`, `deployed`, `target_station`, `pure_tests`, `last_commit`): these describe
    module trees that live in SEPARATE repos (`Cliente/Leon-Guanjuato`, `Cliente/Honeywell/MX60`).
    A `niagara-tools` check cannot see those diffs, so they are RECORDED here, not gated.
- **Unknown is honest.** A DECLARED field with no authoritative value is `unknown`, never a guess.
- **Deployed-baseline fields (optional, DECLARED) — answer "which tree is on the station?" without Software Manager:** `deployed_jar_sha256`, `deployed_build_millis` (the installed jar's `module.xml` `buildMillis`), `deployed_source_commit` (the `module_repo` commit that built it), `deployed_baseline_date`. A `vendorVersion` bump alone does not answer this: a BEHAVIOR-only fix (no schema change) can leave two source trees at the SAME version, and Software Manager reports "Up to Date" against whichever jar is already installed regardless of which tree built it. Record these fields at every deploy so a later session can answer "which tree is actually on the station" from this file instead of comparing candidate checkouts by hand. See the ColdRoomPan canonical example below for the field shape; `unknown` is honest for a deploy that predates this template. `[ev: retro panccadia-defrost-sequencing-hmi-reload-deltas Δ3]` (the gap this template closes is documented at `[ev: retro panccadia-defrost-sequencing-hmi-reload-deltas Δ2]` — the CompPan leon/leon2 ambiguity).
- **`source_of_truth` (DECLARED, required for every module): `<path>@<branch>@<commit>`** — WHICH checkout is authoritative for this module: `<path>` is the client tree (as in `module_repo`), `<branch>` the branch builds come from, `<commit>` the commit last confirmed as the source of record. Update it in the SAME session that moves the authoritative tree, branch or commit — never only in session memory (the authoritative source of one client moved three times in three weeks and sessions kept rediscovering it). `unknown` stays honest, for the whole field or any part (`<path>@unknown@unknown`). `scripts/check-client-source.sh --build-state BUILD-STATE.md --module <MOD> <module-root>` (BUILD-LOOP §0.b) compares a checkout against it. `[ev: retro client-source-of-truth Δ2]`
- **`ui_profile` (DECLARED, required for every module with a `ux` profile): `hmi | lan | both | unknown`** — who opens the dashboard; it selects which profile-tagged rules apply. The values, the `both` single-build/two-entry rule and `unknown` are defined ONCE in `types/frontend-standard.md` § Profiles (single source; `types/dashboard.md` and this file only reference it). Record it in the module's envelope (and its module docs) the first time the `-ux` is touched. `[ev: retro dashboard-deployment-profiles Δ1]`

deployed_jar_sha256: unknown         # DECLARED (optional, Δ3 template) — this session's built jar (2.2.2) is 09829a8b...70de1fc4 (packaged as v7, not installed); the CURRENTLY DEPLOYED jar's hash is unknown — no station read performed this session
deployed_build_millis: unknown       # DECLARED (optional) — installed jar's module.xml buildMillis (`unzip -p <jar> META-INF/module.xml`)

last_session: 2026-09-25 · continuous-fan-post-defrost-delay: T1/T2 (2.2.1, v6) opened the restart-sequencing window unconditionally on exitDefrost() -- caught a same-day regression (T3) BEFORE deploy: air-defrost units (Cuartos 1/2/4, airDefrost=true+continuous+dripTime=0) had their already-running fan needlessly stopped/restarted. Fixed with ColdRoomControl.postDefrostFanHold(airDefrost, fromDrip) gating the hold; version bumped again 2.2.1->2.2.2 (v7) so a fixed and a regressed build never share one version. v6 marked NO INSTALAR, v7 is the version to install. Built+verified+packaged, NOT deployed; next: install v7 on station, confirm Cuarto 3 (electric) fan follows the valve by ~startDelay, confirm Cuartos 1/2/4 (air) fan shows no interruption; then consider the DefrostController pure-test gap above

## kit — the kit's own evolution (self-section)

Kit-infrastructure work (changing the kit itself, not building a module) has no module build to
record, so it updates THIS `kit` self-section. Same `build-state.v1` envelope and same `BUILD-LOOP.md`
§7 close gate — this is the kit's no-registro fix applied to its own evolution.
Its `last_change:`, `open_issues:` items and session log are GENERATED from `ledger.d/` by `toolbelt/gen-build-state.sh`: add a fragment, never edit those lines here.

Split-retro fold rule (from Campaign 2): when a retro's remaining lesson has a LATER PR that will carry it, revert its INDEX row to `pending`; when it has NO later home, fold it now — reverting would orphan the lesson.

(G86 bacnet-export-enum-range, drp Δ5, advisory, later; [deferred: retro devguide415-r2-drivers-protocols Δ5]) scans the same `*.bog` set; rule: WARN when a `bacnet:BacnetMultiState*Descriptor` `pointOrd` resolves to a point whose `facets` `range` (`E:{tag=ordinal,...}`) does not hold exactly the ordinals 1..n, and INFO when the point is unresolvable; row: `WARN bacnet-export-enum-range <bog>:<line> <descriptor> range <ordinals> must be 1..n contiguous`; advisory: exit 0 (1 under --strict), 3 usage, unreadable file or empty root; cases: range `{auto=0,hand=1,off=2}` → WARN; `{a=1,b=3}` → WARN; `{a=2,b=3}` → WARN; `{a=1,b=2,c=3}` → clean; a numeric point behind an analog descriptor → clean; an enum point with no export descriptor → clean. (G88 rdbms-dialect-descriptor, shl Δ8 + r3di Δ6, advisory, later; [deferred: retro devguide415-r2-shipped-libraries Δ8] [deferred: retro devguide415-r3-drivers-integration Δ6]) scans `*.java` under every `-rt` `src/` of one module root, comments and string literals stripped, test sources excluded; rule: WARN on `new B(SqlServer|MySQL|Oracle|HsqlDb)HistoryExport(` (the dialect is fixed in code instead of `getHistories().getExportDescriptorType().getInstance()`), and on a `jdbc:postgresql:` string literal (Postgres has no shipped dialect) when the module has no class extending `BRdbms`; row: `WARN rdbms-dialect-descriptor <file>:<line> <hard-coded dialect descriptor|postgres jdbc url>`; advisory: exit 0 (1 under --strict), 3 usage, unreadable tree or empty root; cases: `new BSqlServerHistoryExport()` → WARN; `new BMySQLHistoryExport()` → WARN; `"jdbc:postgresql://db/x"` in a module without a `BRdbms` subclass → WARN; `db.getHistories().getExportDescriptorType().getInstance()` → clean; the same `new` in a comment → clean; `jdbc:postgresql:` in a module that ships its own `BRdbms` dialect → clean.
